# what each instruction of Arm's instruction model does beyond what its operands state, one line per fact
#
# the instruction model (AARCHMRS) states no instruction's meaning, so every fact here is read from the instruction's
# page of the A64 ISA XML 2026-09, its decode and execute pseudocode under the bits the encoding fixes, cited by its
# file, and no line overrides the model. an instruction with no line of a kind has none of that kind, and every
# instruction has a latency line
#
#   flags <instruction> | <read|write|read-write>
#     its reads and writes of PSTATE.N, Z, C and V. a write of some of the four keeps the rest, so it reads them
#   implicit <instruction> | [read=<registers>] [written=<registers>]
#     the registers it reads and writes that no operand names, by the register file's names, as bl's x30 and a
#     floating point instruction's fpcr and fpsr. a register read only under a state the encoding does not fix, as
#     reta's x16 under PSTATE.PACM, is read. a write keeping some of a register's old value, as an accrued fpsr flag or
#     a slice of za, reads it too
#   control <instruction> | transfer=<jump|call|return|trap> [returns=<yes|no>] target=<direct|indirect|untargeted>
#                           [conditional=yes] [arg=<rule>]
#     its transfer of control and the operand holding a jump's or call's target. a return goes where its caller left,
#     whichever register holds it, so it is untargeted. a trap states whether its handler resumes at the next
#     instruction (gen.control): svc, hvc and smc return to it, brk once a debugger steps past it, and hlt on leaving
#     debug state, as semihosting's hlt #0xf000 returns its result, and udf's undefined instruction exception does not
#   memory <instruction> | role=<read|write|read-write> base=<rule> [forming=<rules>] [governor=<rule>]
#                          [count=<unstated|rule>] width=<width>
#   memory <instruction> | role=... base=... by=<fields> <bits>=<width> ...
#     one access, through the address its base operand holds or, for a literal load, is relative to. `forming` are the
#     further operands forming the address, a rule a row may leave out, `governor` the predicate choosing which
#     elements it touches, and `count` the operand whose value counts its elements where that is not one, `unstated`
#     where no operand does, as a gather's active elements or a tag block's. the width is bits, or
#     `<fixed>+<per>*<slot>` for one scaling with a slot, or by the bits of fields the rows fix, joined by `:`
#   offset <instruction> | to=<rule> from=<rule> [by=<rule>] [negated=yes] [shift=<rule> step=<bits>]
#     the value it writes as the value it reads plus an immediate, shifted left by step bits for each unit of the
#     shift operand's value (gen.relation), or `to=<rule> formed=<rule>` for one it forms from a label and its own
#     address
#   latency <instruction> | <one|few|many|serial>
#     masc's latency class, which no page states
#   order <instruction|group> | [before=<kinds>] [after=<kinds>] [field=<fields> bits=<pattern>]
#     how it orders the accesses around it (gen.order), of one instruction or of every instruction of a group of the
#     model by its last name, read from the model's named fields where the encoding carries it, joined by `:`
#   ordered <instruction|group> | <atomic|fence>
#     an instruction, or every instruction of a group, that must state an order fact
